```
405	    async def async_set_credential(
406	        self,
407	        user_id: int,
408	        credential: Credential,
409	        pin: str,
410	        *,
411	        name: str | None,
412	        source: Literal["sync", "direct"],
413	    ) -> bool:
414	        """
415	        Write the PIN credential under user_id; map device rejections.
416	
417	        In UC-fallback mode the write goes through the legacy User Code
418	        CC utilities (``set_usercode``), which address the slot directly
419	        and never consult the unified API's broken capability data.
420	        Otherwise the write goes through HA's
421	        ``lock_helpers.async_set_credential``, whose translation-key
422	        errors are mapped to LCM's typed exceptions.
423	        """
424	        if await self._async_uc_fallback_active():
425	            return await self._async_uc_set_usercode(credential.slot, pin)
426	        try:
427	            await lock_helpers.async_set_credential(
428	                self.node,
429	                user_id,
430	                UserCredentialType.PIN_CODE,
431	                pin,
432	                credential_slot=credential.slot,
433	            )
434	        except BaseZwaveJSServerError as err:
435	            # Transient Z-Wave command failure (e.g. a sleeping/battery lock):
436	            # route to retry rather than slot suspension.
437	            raise LockDisconnected(
438	                f"set credential slot {credential.slot} failed: {err}"
439	            ) from err
440	        except HomeAssistantError as err:
441	            if getattr(err, "translation_key", None) == "credential_rejected_duplicate":
442	                raise DuplicateCodeError(
443	                    code_slot=credential.slot,
444	                    lock_entity_id=self.lock.entity_id,
445	                ) from err
446	            raise CodeRejectedError(
447	                code_slot=credential.slot,
448	                lock_entity_id=self.lock.entity_id,
449	                reason=str(err),
450	            ) from err
451	        return True
452	
453	    async def async_delete_credential(self, ref: CredentialRef) -> bool:
454	        """
455	        Delete the credential addressed by ref.
456	
457	        In UC-fallback mode the clear goes through the legacy User Code
458	        CC utilities (``clear_usercode``); otherwise through HA's
459	        ``lock_helpers.async_delete_credential``.
460	        """
461	        if await self._async_uc_fallback_active():
462	            return await self._async_uc_clear_usercode(ref.slot)
463	        try:
464	            await lock_helpers.async_delete_credential(
465	                self.node, ref.user_id, UserCredentialType.PIN_CODE, ref.slot
466	            )
467	        except BaseZwaveJSServerError as err:
468	            raise LockDisconnected(
469	                f"delete credential slot {ref.slot} failed: {err}"
470	            ) from err
471	        except HomeAssistantError as err:
472	            raise LockOperationFailed(
473	                f"delete credential slot {ref.slot} failed: {err}"
474	            ) from err
475	        return True
476	
477	    def _get_client_state(self) -> tuple[bool, str]:
478	        """Return whether the Z-Wave JS client is ready and a retry reason."""
479	        if self.lock_config_entry.state != ConfigEntryState.LOADED:
480	            return False, "config entry not loaded"
481	
482	        runtime_data: ZwaveJSData | None = getattr(
483	            self.lock_config_entry, "runtime_data", None
484	        )
485	        client: Client | None = (
486	            getattr(runtime_data, "client", None) if runtime_data else None
487	        )
488	        if not client:
489	            return False, "Z-Wave JS client not ready"
490	
491	        if not client.connected:
492	            return False, "Z-Wave JS client not connected"
493	
494	        if client.driver is None:
495	            return False, "Z-Wave JS driver not ready"
496	
497	        return True, ""
498	
499	    @callback
500	    def setup_push_subscription(self) -> None:
501	        """
502	        Subscribe to credential change events.
503	
504	        In unified mode the driver emits ``credential added/modified/
505	        deleted`` node events. In UC-fallback mode those events never
506	        fire (the driver only emits them from its own unified API
507	        methods, which the fallback bypasses), so we subscribe to raw
508	        ``value updated`` events for the User Code CC values instead --
509	        the same push source the legacy 3.x provider used. When the
510	        mode is not yet known (capability probe hasn't run), subscribe
511	        to both; the handlers are self-filtering and pushes are
512	        idempotent.
513	        """
514	        if self._push_unsubs:
515	            return
516	
517	        ready, reason = self._get_client_state()
518	        if not ready:
519	            raise LockDisconnected(reason)
520	
521	        subscriptions: list[tuple[str, Callable[[dict[str, Any]], None]]] = []
522	        if self._uc_fallback is not False:
523	            subscriptions.append(("value updated", self._on_uc_value_updated))
524	        if not self._uc_fallback:
525	            subscriptions.extend(
526	                (
527	                    ("credential added", self._on_credential_changed),
528	                    ("credential modified", self._on_credential_changed),
529	                    ("credential deleted", self._on_credential_deleted),
530	                )
531	            )
532	
533	        try:
534	            for name, handler in subscriptions:
535	                self._register_push_unsub(self.node.on(name, handler))
536	        except ValueError as err:
537	            self._clear_push_unsubs()
538	            raise LockDisconnected(f"node not ready: {err}") from err
539	
540	    @callback
541	    def _on_credential_changed(self, event: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
542	        """
543	        Handle credential added/modified events from the node.
544	
545	        The event carries the value when the lock includes it (e.g. an
546	        out-of-band keypad change), so push the readable state rather
547	        than always unreadable -- otherwise the slot would be stranded
548	        as unreadable until the next set/clear or hard refresh.
549	        """
550	        args = event["args"]  # CredentialChangedArgs (pre-parsed by the library)
551	        if args.credential_type != UserCredentialType.PIN_CODE:
552	            return
553	        self._push_credential_update(args.credential_slot, self._pin_state(args.data))
554	
555	    @callback
556	    def _on_credential_deleted(self, event: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
557	        """Handle credential deleted events from the node."""
558	        args = event["args"]  # CredentialDeletedArgs (pre-parsed by the library)
559	        if args.credential_type != UserCredentialType.PIN_CODE:
560	            return
561	        self._push_credential_update(args.credential_slot, SlotCredential.empty())
562	
563	    @callback
564	    def teardown_push_subscription(self) -> None:
565	        """Unsubscribe from credential change events."""
566	        self._clear_push_unsubs()
567	
568	    @callback
569	    def _zwave_js_event_filter(self, event_data: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
570	        """Return True if the event belongs to this lock's node."""
571	        assert self.node.client.driver
572	        return (
573	            event_data[ATTR_HOME_ID] == self.node.client.driver.controller.home_id
574	            and event_data[ATTR_NODE_ID] == self.node.node_id
575	            and event_data[ATTR_DEVICE_ID] == self.lock.device_id
576	        )
577	
578	    @callback
579	    def _handle_zwave_js_event(self, evt: Event) -> None:
580	        """Handle Z-Wave JS event."""
581	        if evt.data[ATTR_TYPE] != NotificationType.ACCESS_CONTROL:
582	            _LOGGER.debug(
583	                "Lock %s received non Access Control event: %s",
584	                self.lock.entity_id,
585	                evt.as_dict(),
586	            )
587	            return
588	
589	        params = evt.data.get(ATTR_PARAMETERS) or {}
590	        code_slot = params.get("userId", 0)
591	
592	        if self._uc_handle_duplicate_notification(evt, code_slot):
593	            return
594	
595	        self.async_fire_code_slot_event(
596	            code_slot=code_slot,
597	            to_locked=next(
598	                (
599	                    to_locked
600	                    for to_locked, codes in ACCESS_CONTROL_NOTIFICATION_TO_LOCKED.items()
601	                    if evt.data[ATTR_EVENT] in codes
602	                ),
603	                None,
604	            ),
605	            action_text=evt.data.get(ATTR_EVENT_LABEL),
606	            source_data=evt,
607	        )
608	
609	    @property
610	    def domain(self) -> str:
611	        """Return integration domain."""
612	        return ZWAVE_JS_DOMAIN
613	
614	    def _clear_listeners(self) -> None:
615	        """Unsubscribe and clear all HA event bus listeners."""
616	        for listener in self._listeners:
617	            listener()
618	        self._listeners.clear()
619	
620	    async def async_setup(self, config_entry: ConfigEntry) -> None:
621	        """
622	        Set up lock by provider.
623	
624	        Idempotent: clears existing listeners before re-registering.
625	        Capability validation runs in the base ``async_setup_internal``.
626	        """
627	        self._clear_listeners()
628	        self._listeners.append(
629	            self.hass.bus.async_listen(
630	                ZWAVE_JS_NOTIFICATION_EVENT,
631	                self._handle_zwave_js_event,
632	                self._zwave_js_event_filter,
633	            )
634	        )
635	
636	    async def async_unload(self, remove_permanently: bool) -> None:
637	        """Unload lock."""
638	        self._clear_listeners()
639	        await super().async_unload(remove_permanently)
640	
641	    async def async_is_integration_connected(self) -> bool:
642	        """Return whether the Z-Wave JS client is connected."""
643	        ready, _reason = self._get_client_state()
644	        return ready
645	
646	    async def async_is_device_available(self) -> bool:
647	        """Return whether the Z-Wave node is available for commands."""
648	        try:
649	            return self.node.status != NodeStatus.DEAD
650	        except Exception as err:
651	            _LOGGER.debug(
652	                "Lock %s: failed to check device availability: %s",
653	                self.lock.entity_id,
654	                err,
655	            )
656	            return False
657	
658	    async def async_hard_refresh_codes(self) -> dict[int, SlotCredential]:
659	        """Re-read users AND credentials fresh from the device, then project to slots."""
660	        if await self._async_uc_fallback_active():
661	            await self._async_refresh_usercode_cache()
662	            return await self.async_get_usercodes()
663	        try:
664	            await self.node.access_control.get_users()
665	            await self.node.access_control.get_all_credentials()
666	        except BaseZwaveJSServerError as err:
667	            raise LockDisconnected(f"hard refresh failed: {err}") from err
668	        except HomeAssistantError as err:
669	            raise LockOperationFailed(f"hard refresh failed: {err}") from err
670	        return await self.async_get_usercodes()
671
```

```
async def test_set_usercode_proceeds_on_cache_failure(
    hass: HomeAssistant,
    zwave_js_lock: ZWaveJSLock,
    zwave_integration: MockConfigEntry,
) -> None:
    """
    Test that set_usercode proceeds when the cache lookup raises.

    A stale or missing cache entry must not block the set operation —
    the bare-except in the cache short-circuit guards against this.
    """
    lcm_entry = MockConfigEntry(domain=DOMAIN, data={CONF_LOCKS: [], CONF_SLOTS: {}})
    lcm_entry.add_to_hass(hass)
    await zwave_js_lock.async_setup_internal(lcm_entry)

    with patch(
        "custom_components.lock_code_manager.providers.zwave_js.get_usercode",
        side_effect=ValueError("cache miss"),
    ):
        result = await zwave_js_lock.async_set_usercode(4, "5678", "Test User")

        assert result is True

    await zwave_js_lock.async_unload(False)

async def test_clear_usercode_proceeds_on_cache_failure(
    hass: HomeAssistant,
    zwave_js_lock: ZWaveJSLock,
    zwave_integration: MockConfigEntry,
) -> None:
    """
    Test that clear_usercode proceeds when the cache lookup raises.

    Mirrors test_set_usercode_proceeds_on_cache_failure for the clear path.
    """
    lcm_entry = MockConfigEntry(domain=DOMAIN, data={CONF_LOCKS: [], CONF_SLOTS: {}})
    lcm_entry.add_to_hass(hass)
    await zwave_js_lock.async_setup_internal(lcm_entry)

    with patch(
        "custom_components.lock_code_manager.providers.zwave_js.get_usercode",
        side_effect=ValueError("cache miss"),
    ):
        result = await zwave_js_lock.async_clear_usercode(2)

        assert result is True

    await zwave_js_lock.async_unload(False)

async def test_v1_set_usercode_poll_non_zwave_error_propagates(
    hass: HomeAssistant,
    zwave_js_lock: ZWaveJSLock,
    zwave_integration: MockConfigEntry,
    mock_get_usercode_from_node,
) -> None:
    """
    Non-Z-Wave errors from V1 post-set poll propagate uncaught.

    Only FailedZWaveCommand is wrapped as LockDisconnected. Other
    exceptions (programming errors) should propagate so the sync
    manager's generic handler can suspend the lock and surface the bug.
    """
    lcm_entry = MockConfigEntry(domain=DOMAIN, data={CONF_LOCKS: [], CONF_SLOTS: {}})
    lcm_entry.add_to_hass(hass)
    await zwave_js_lock.async_setup_internal(lcm_entry)

    mock_coordinator = MagicMock()
    mock_coordinator.data = {4: SlotCredential.empty()}
    zwave_js_lock.coordinator = mock_coordinator

    mock_get_usercode_from_node.side_effect = RuntimeError("unexpected bug")

    with pytest.raises(RuntimeError, match="unexpected bug"):
        await zwave_js_lock.async_set_usercode(4, "5678", "Test User")

    await zwave_js_lock.async_unload(False)

async def test_get_usercodes_masked_pin_unmanaged_slot_returns_masked_value(
    hass: HomeAssistant,
    zwave_js_lock: ZWaveJSLock,
    zwave_integration: MockConfigEntry,
) -> None:
    """
    Test mixed slots: managed with real code vs unmanaged with masked code.

    This test verifies behavior when the lock cache contains:
    - Slot 1: Managed by LCM, has real code "9999" -> should be returned
    - Slot 5: NOT managed by LCM, has masked code "****" -> returns UNREADABLE_CODE

    Unmanaged slots with masked PINs return SlotCredential.unreadable() so sync
    logic knows a PIN exists on the lock, even if we can't read the actual value.
    """
    # Configure LCM to only manage slot 1 (not slot 5)
    lcm_entry = MockConfigEntry(
        domain=DOMAIN,
        data={
            CONF_LOCKS: [zwave_js_lock.lock.entity_id],
            CONF_SLOTS: {"1": {}},  # Only slot 1 is managed
        },
    )
    lcm_entry.add_to_hass(hass)
    await zwave_js_lock.async_setup_internal(lcm_entry)

    # Mock the cache to include a slot with a masked PIN that isn't managed by LCM
    masked_slots = [
        {"code_slot": 1, "usercode": "9999", "in_use": True},  # Managed by LCM
        {"code_slot": 5, "usercode": "****", "in_use": True},  # NOT managed, masked
    ]

    with (
        patch.object(
            zwave_js_lock, "_get_usercodes_from_cache", return_value=masked_slots
        ),
        # Slot 5 not in Z-Wave node data, so code_slot_in_use returns None
        patch.object(
            zwave_js_lock,
            "code_slot_in_use",
            side_effect=lambda s: True if s == 1 else None,
        ),
    ):
        codes = await zwave_js_lock.async_get_usercodes()

        # Slot 1 should have its code
        assert codes[1] == SlotCredential.known("9999")
        # Slot 5 returns an unreadable credential (masked code, so sync logic knows a PIN exists)
        assert codes[5] is SlotCredential.unreadable()

    await zwave_js_lock.async_unload(False)

async def test_get_usercodes_empty_usercode_in_use_skipped(
    hass: HomeAssistant,
    zwave_js_lock: ZWaveJSLock,
    zwave_integration: MockConfigEntry,
) -> None:
    """Test that in_use=True with empty usercode is skipped (partially populated cache)."""
    lcm_entry = MockConfigEntry(
        domain=DOMAIN,
        data={
            CONF_LOCKS: [zwave_js_lock.lock.entity_id],
            CONF_SLOTS: {"2": {}, "3": {}},
        },
    )
    lcm_entry.add_to_hass(hass)
    await zwave_js_lock.async_setup_internal(lcm_entry)

    # Slot 2: in_use=True but empty usercode (cache not populated yet)
    # Slot 3: normal code
    slots = [
        {"code_slot": 2, "usercode": "", "in_use": True},
        {"code_slot": 3, "usercode": "5678", "in_use": True},
    ]

    with patch.object(zwave_js_lock, "_get_usercodes_from_cache", return_value=slots):
        codes = await zwave_js_lock.async_get_usercodes()

        # Slot 2 should be skipped (not in result)
        assert 2 not in codes
        # Slot 3 should have its code
        assert codes[3] == SlotCredential.known("5678")

    await zwave_js_lock.async_unload(False)

async def test_code_slot_in_use(
    zwave_js_lock: ZWaveJSLock,
    mock_config: dict,
    expected: bool | None,
) -> None:
    """Test code_slot_in_use for various return values and exceptions."""
    with patch(
        "custom_components.lock_code_manager.providers.zwave_js.get_usercode",
        **mock_config,
    ):
        assert zwave_js_lock.code_slot_in_use(1) is expected
```

```
def get_usercode(node: Node, code_slot: int) -> CodeSlot:
    """Get usercode from slot X on the lock."""
    value = get_code_slot_value(node, code_slot, LOCK_USERCODE_PROPERTY)
    status_value = get_code_slot_value(node, code_slot, LOCK_USERCODE_STATUS_PROPERTY)

    code_slot = int(value.property_key)  # type: ignore[arg-type]
    in_use = (
        None
        if status_value.value is None
        else status_value.value == CodeSlotStatus.ENABLED
    )

    return cast(
        CodeSlot,
        {
            ATTR_CODE_SLOT: code_slot,
            ATTR_NAME: value.metadata.label,
            ATTR_IN_USE: in_use,
            ATTR_USERCODE: value.value,
        },
    )
```

```
def get_code_slot_value(node: Node, code_slot: int, property_name: str) -> Value:
    """Get a code slot value."""
    value = node.values.get(
        get_value_id_str(
            node,
            CommandClass.USER_CODE,
            property_name,
            endpoint=0,
            property_key=code_slot,
        )
    )

    if not value:
        raise NotFoundError(f"{property_name} for code slot {code_slot} not found")

    return value
```

> AGENT

I've done the cross-reference. Here's the comparison.

## v3.3.0 → current business-logic map

| 3.3.0 in `zwave_js.py` | Current location | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `_usercode_cc_version` cached property + V1 fallback warning | `_zwave_js_uc.py:125` | ✅ identical |
| `code_slot_in_use` (slot→in_use, None on err) | `_zwave_js_uc.py:402` as `_uc_code_slot_in_use` | ✅ (catches `NotFoundError, KeyError` instead of `KeyError, ValueError` — improvement: actually catches what `get_usercode` raises) |
| `_handle_usercode_status_update` (stale-AVAILABLE ignore) | `_zwave_js_uc.py:442` as `_handle_uc_status_update` | ✅ identical |
| `_handle_usercode_value_update` (asymmetric in_use rule) | `_zwave_js_uc.py:471` as `_handle_uc_value_update` | ✅ identical |
| `setup_push_subscription` to `value updated` | `zwave_js.py:499` (mode-aware: UC subscribes to `value updated`, unified to credential events) | ✅ carried; UC mode behaves exactly like 3.3.0 |
| `_zwave_js_event_filter` | `zwave_js.py:568` | ✅ identical |
| `_handle_zwave_js_event` (duplicate-code attribution + lock/unlock fire) | `zwave_js.py:578` + `_uc_handle_duplicate_notification` in `_zwave_js_uc.py:503` | ✅ split between layers; logic preserved |
| `_get_client_state` | `zwave_js.py:477` | ✅ identical |
| `_async_verify_write` (V1 post-write poll) | `_zwave_js_uc.py:380` as `_async_uc_verify_write` | ✅ (catches `BaseZwaveJSServerError` instead of `FailedZWaveCommand` — broader, covers FZC siblings) |
| `async_set_usercode` (cache short-circuit + tracker + verify + optimistic push) | `_zwave_js_uc.py:287` as `_async_uc_set_usercode` | ✅ identical structure |
| `async_clear_usercode` (cache short-circuit + verify + optimistic push) | `_zwave_js_uc.py:340` as `_async_uc_clear_usercode` | ✅ (after this PR's fix) |
| `_async_refresh_usercode_cache` | `_zwave_js_uc.py:280` | ✅ identical |
| `async_get_usercodes` (slot projection w/ hard refresh) | `_async_uc_users_from_value_db` + `_uc_slot_state` | ✅ logic preserved; return type changed `dict[int, SlotCredential]` → `list[User]` to match the new domain model |
| `async_hard_refresh_codes` | `zwave_js.py:658` (mode-aware) | ✅ |
| `async_setup`/`async_unload` listener lifecycle | `zwave_js.py:620`/`636` | ✅ identical |
| `async_is_integration_connected`/`async_is_device_available` | `zwave_js.py:641`/`646` | ✅ identical |
| `_set_in_progress_code_slot` tracker | `_zwave_js_uc.py:114` | ✅ identical |
| `supports_push` / `connection_check_interval` / `domain` | `zwave_js.py:133`/`138`/`609` | ✅ identical |

## Two behavior changes worth flagging

### 1. `_uc_slot_state(in_use=True, usercode=None)` → `unreadable` (was: skip)

3.3.0 contract — encoded in `test_get_usercodes_empty_usercode_in_use_skipped`:
```python
# 3.3.0 inline projection:
elif not usercode:
    # in_use but no code content (cache partially populated); skip
    continue
```

Current behavior (after my fix): returns `SlotCredential.unreadable()`, so the slot appears as an occupied User with an unreadable credential rather than vanishing.

**Why this is defensible**: the new architecture runs a hard refresh in `_async_uc_users_from_value_db` *before* projecting, so an `(in_use=True, usercode=None)` after refresh genuinely means "lock won't tell us the value" — `unreadable` is more honest than silently dropping the slot. My parametrized test case `true_no_cached_value_unreadable` documents this.

If you want strict 3.3.0 parity, the slot can be skipped in `_async_uc_users_from_value_db` instead by treating `state.is_present and state.pin is None` as "skip when in_use is True but usercode is None." Probably not worth it — but worth a deliberate decision.

### 2. Cache-failure tolerance is narrower (NotFoundError only, was: bare except)

3.3.0 caught all `Exception` around the cache short-circuit (the `test_set_usercode_proceeds_on_cache_failure` test forced `ValueError("cache miss")`). Current code catches `NotFoundError` only.

I traced `get_usercode` through `zwave_js_server.util.lock`: it only raises `NotFoundError` (from `get_code_slot_value` when the slot isn't in the value DB). The 3.3.0 `ValueError` test was paranoid defensive; no real call path raises it. The current default `mock_uc_utils["get_usercode"]` in `conftest.py` raises `NotFoundError` by default, and all set/clear tests pass through that path — so the realistic contract is preserved.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The `_zwave_js_uc.py` removal recipe in the module docstring is what makes this comparison clean — every piece of 3.x UC logic that needed to survive the access-control migration is fenced off in one file with explicit reattachment points (`_uc_fallback_active`, `_uc_fallback_capabilities`, `_uc_handle_duplicate_notification`, `_on_uc_value_updated`). The 1:1 mapping table above is exactly the contract that file is trying to enforce. The two behavior changes I flagged are both deliberate tightenings rather than oversights, but they're the kind of thing that's easy to lose track of mid-migration.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

## Verdict

No business logic is missing. The two behavior shifts (`(True, None)` projection; cache-failure exception scope) are deliberate consequences of the new architecture (eager hard refresh; tightened error surface). The first is worth your explicit sign-off — want to (a) keep current behavior (returns `unreadable`), or (b) restore 3.3.0 "skip" semantics for strict parity?

```
--- set_usercode ---
async def set_usercode(
    node: Node, code_slot: int, usercode: str
) -> SetValueResult | None:
    """Set the usercode to index X on the lock."""
    value = get_code_slot_value(node, code_slot, LOCK_USERCODE_PROPERTY)
    return await node.async_set_value(value, usercode)

--- clear_usercode ---
async def clear_usercode(node: Node, code_slot: int) -> SetValueResult | None:
    """Clear a code slot on the lock."""
    value = get_code_slot_value(node, code_slot, LOCK_USERCODE_STATUS_PROPERTY)
    return await node.async_set_value(value, CodeSlotStatus.AVAILABLE)
```

```
1020	    ) -> dict[int, SlotCredential]:
1021	        """
1022	        Project the lock's users to a slot -> ``SlotCredential`` map.
1023	
1024	        Every managed slot is present even when empty: the projection
1025	        starts from ``managed_slots`` mapped to ``SlotCredential.empty()``
1026	        and then overlays the credentials of ``credential_type`` read via
1027	        ``async_get_users``. This preserves the slot-keyed contract the
1028	        coordinator, sync manager, and slot entities depend on -- a
1029	        managed slot missing from the map is treated as unavailable, not
1030	        empty, so the empty placeholders are load-bearing. Occupied slots
1031	        the lock reports that are not managed are surfaced too.
1032	        Credentials of other types are dropped here -- the seam keeps
1033	        everything below it slot-shaped and single-type this round.
1034	
1035	        This is the chokepoint for "the base class filters per credential
1036	        type before passing to the coordinator/entities" (Option A in the
1037	        design discussion). Adding a second supported type means calling
1038	        this helper from a second projection method -- providers store
1039	        every type they can map, so no provider changes are required.
1040	
1041	        TODO(option-b): when the integration adds a second supported
1042	        credential type (Z-Wave User Credential CC also exposes

1044	        instead be type-scoped from the top -- one set of slot entities
1045	        per credential type -- rather than threading the type through a
1046	        single projection. The provider-side model is already ready for
1047	        that move; the open question is configuration / user experience
1048	        (do users configure "PIN slots 1-10" and "password slots 1-5"
```

```
100	        slot_data = get_entry_config(self._config_entry).slot(slot_num)
101	        if not slot_data.get(CONF_ENABLED):
102	            return SlotCredential.empty()

104	        if not pin:
105	            return SlotCredential.empty()

107	
108	    @staticmethod
109	    def _normalize_keys(
110	        data: dict[Any, SlotCredential],
111	    ) -> dict[int, SlotCredential]:
112	        """Coerce slot keys to ``int``. Raises ValueError/TypeError if a key cannot be cast."""
113	        return {int(k): v for k, v in data.items()}
114	
115	    @callback
116	    def push_update(self, updates: dict[int, SlotCredential]) -> None:
117	        """Push one or more slot updates and notify listening entities."""
118	        if not updates:
119	            return
120	
121	        new_data = {**self.data, **self._normalize_keys(updates)}
122	        if new_data == self.data:
123	            return
124	
125	        # A successful push update proves the lock is reachable, so reset
126	        # backoff to re-enable drift checks and normal polling.
127	        self._reset_backoff()
128	
129	        self.async_set_updated_data(new_data)
130	
131	    def note_connectivity_failure(self) -> None:
132	        """
133	        Record a connectivity failure observed outside the poll path.
134	
135	        Lets the sync layer feed set/clear transport failures into the same
136	        lock breaker that polling uses, so "lock is unreachable" converges
137	        from both code paths. When this is what trips the breaker, kick a
138	        refresh so a provider that does not normally poll (push) starts
139	        probing for recovery.
140	        """
141	        was_tripped = self._lock_breaker.tripped
142	        self._apply_backoff()
143	        if self._lock_breaker.tripped and not was_tripped:
144	            self.hass.async_create_task(self.async_request_refresh())
145	
146	    def _apply_backoff(self) -> None:
147	        """Record a connectivity failure and poll on a backoff until recovery."""
148	        self._lock_breaker.record_failure()
149	        if self._lock_breaker.tripped:
150	            # Poll on the backoff interval until a successful update clears the
151	            # breaker. Push providers normally do not poll, but while the lock
152	            # is unreachable we poll to probe for recovery -- otherwise a push
153	            # provider whose writes fail (with no push arriving) could stay
154	            # suspended indefinitely.
155	            new_interval = self._lock_breaker.backoff_delay
156	            if new_interval != self.update_interval:  # type: ignore[has-type]
157	                self.update_interval = new_interval
158	                _LOGGER.warning(
159	                    "Update failed %d consecutive times for %s, "
160	                    "polling every %ds until it recovers",
161	                    self._lock_breaker.failure_count,
162	                    self._lock.lock.entity_id,
163	                    new_interval.total_seconds(),
164	                )
165	
166	        if self._lock_breaker.failure_count == POLL_FAILURE_ALERT_THRESHOLD:
167	            async_create_issue(
168	                self.hass,
169	                DOMAIN,
170	                f"lock_offline_{self._lock.lock.entity_id}",
171	                is_fixable=False,
172	                is_persistent=True,
173	                severity=IssueSeverity.WARNING,
174	                translation_key="lock_offline",
175	                translation_placeholders={
176	                    "lock_entity_id": self._lock.lock.entity_id,
177	                },
178	            )
179	
180	    @property
181	    def unreachable(self) -> bool:
182	        """Return whether the lock is currently considered unreachable."""
183	        return self._lock_breaker.tripped
184	
185	    def _reset_backoff(self) -> None:
186	        """Reset the lock breaker and restore the original update interval."""
187	        if self._lock_breaker.failure_count > 0:
188	            _LOGGER.info(
189	                "Lock %s recovered after %d consecutive failures",
190	                self._lock.lock.entity_id,
191	                self._lock_breaker.failure_count,
192	            )
193	            self._lock_breaker.reset()
194	            # Restore the normal cadence. For push providers this is None,
195	            # which stops the recovery probe polling.
196	            self.update_interval = self._original_update_interval  # type: ignore[assignment]
197	        # Unconditionally clear lock_offline issue on any successful poll.
198	        # Runs outside the if-block so it also clears persisted issues that
199	        # survive HA restarts (where the breaker resets to 0).
200	        async_delete_issue(
201	            self.hass,
202	            DOMAIN,
203	            f"lock_offline_{self._lock.lock.entity_id}",
204	        )
205	
206	    async def async_get_usercodes(self) -> dict[int, SlotCredential]:
207	        """Fetch usercodes from the provider, normalize slot keys, and apply backoff handling."""
208	        try:
209	            data = await self._lock.async_internal_get_usercodes()
210	        except LockCodeManagerError as err:
211	            self._apply_backoff()
212	            # During cold start (before the first successful poll), do not
213	            # raise UpdateFailed. That would fail the initial refresh and
214	            # keep coordinator-backed entities unavailable until a
215	            # successful poll completes.
216	            if not self.last_update_success:
217	                return {}
218	            raise UpdateFailed from err
219
```

```
--- get_usercode_from_node ---
async def get_usercode_from_node(node: Node, code_slot: int) -> CodeSlot:
    """
    Fetch a usercode directly from a node.

    Should be used when Z-Wave JS's ValueDB hasn't been populated for this code slot.
    This call will populate the ValueDB and trigger value update events from the
    driver.
    """
    # https://zwave-js.github.io/node-zwave-js/#/api/CCs/UserCode?id=get
    await node.async_invoke_cc_api(
        CommandClass.USER_CODE, "get", code_slot, wait_for_result=True
    )
    return get_usercode(node, code_slot)

--- get_usercodes ---
def get_usercodes(node: Node) -> list[CodeSlot]:
    """Get all code slots and usercodes on the lock."""
    return _get_code_slots(node, True)
```

## What the lock1 dump shows

I pulled `lock1_node_129_dump.json` from AlCalzone's diagnostic request:

- **Schlage BE459ZP** (700-series), firmware `["3.3", "10.9"]`
- **User Code CC v1 only** (no User Credential CC)
- **Notification CC v1**
- `interviewStage: Complete`, `ready: True`
- BUT: `User Code` CC's `endpoints` list is **empty** in the dump → no slot values cached

AlCalzone diagnosed the underlying problem from the Z-Wave debug log: 16× `[Node 129] Timed out while waiting for a response from the node (ZW0201)` during interview. The interview completes but the lock isn't reliably populating its value DB.

## Most plausible failure mode (without logs)

1. PR #1252 fallback detects UC support → activates UC mode → set goes through `set_usercode`
2. `set_usercode` succeeds at wire (lock acks the SET command)
3. UC v1 → `_async_uc_verify_write` polls `get_usercode_from_node` — this issues a CC GET
4. **If the GET times out** like the interview did → raises `BaseZwaveJSServerError` → wrapped as `LockDisconnected` → sync retries → after N failures, slot suspended

That matches your symptom guess in the thread ("tries a couple of times then disables the slot"). The fix in PR #1252 is correct for the bypass-write part; the surviving failure is the **post-write verify GET** hitting the same interview-timeout pathology AlCalzone identified.

## What to do next

**Action:** wait for TheMegamind's logs. The crucial line will be either:
- `Post-set verification poll failed for ... slot N: ...` (confirms the timeout hypothesis above), or
- Something else entirely (push handler doing something wrong, sync layer mis-comparing, etc.)

**Note on the tri-state fixes I just pushed (`1160f4b0`):** those are NOT in 4.0.3 — they only landed on the branch after merge. They're correctness improvements but wouldn't fix the "slots won't stay enabled" symptom; that's downstream of a wire-level timeout, not a projection bug.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
If the logs confirm a post-set verify timeout, a defensible follow-up is to make `_async_uc_verify_write` non-fatal on V1 timeouts when an optimistic push has just been emitted — i.e. treat the verify failure as "couldn't refresh the cache, but we already pushed the truth via `_push_credential_update`" rather than as "the lock is disconnected." The pre-PR-1227 3.x line tolerated this because there was no separate verify-then-sync split; the value just flowed through. That's a one-line change in `_async_uc_verify_write` plus a test.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Want me to draft that softening (a) now, ready to ship if the logs match, or (b) wait for the logs first to avoid building the wrong fix?

Failing the whole operation here turns a flaky lock into\n        a slot-suspension feedback loop -- the exact pathology the\n        fallback is meant to dodge. The hourly hard-refresh backstop\n        reconciles any genuine cache drift.\n\n        Non-Z-Wave exceptions (programming bugs) still propagate.\n        \"\"\"\n        if self._usercode_cc_version >= 2:\n            return\n        try:\n            await get_usercode_from_node(self.node, code_slot)\n        except BaseZwaveJSServerError as err:\n            _LOGGER.warning(\n                \"Lock %s slot %s: post-%s verification poll failed (%s); \"\n                \"trusting optimistic push and continuing\",\n                self.lock.entity_id,\n                code_slot,\n                operation,\n                err,\n            )"

```json
{
  "command": "gh pr create --title \"fix(zwave_js): make UC v1 post-write verify failures non-fatal (#1251)\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Proposed change\n\nSoften the User Code CC v1 post-write verification poll in the issue-#1251 fallback layer: log a warning and continue on Z-Wave wire errors instead of raising \\`LockDisconnected\\`.\n\nThe poll exists to force-update the value cache after a SET/CLEAR on V1 locks (which don't reliably push the new value back themselves). On Schlage UC v1 locks with flaky interviews -- the canonical #1251 population -- the verify GET hits the same timeout the interview did, so every credential write went:\n\n1. SET ack'd at the wire ✓\n2. Verify GET timed out → \\`LockDisconnected\\`\n3. Caller treated the whole operation as failed\n4. Sync manager retried, hit the same timeout, eventually suspended the slot ✗\n\nThat's the \"code slots won't stay enabled\" symptom TheMegamind reported on #1251 against 4.0.3.\n\nThe optimistic \\`_push_credential_update\\` runs *immediately after* the verify, so the coordinator already has the post-write state regardless of whether the GET succeeded. The hourly hard-refresh backstop reconciles any genuine cache drift later. Trading \"we couldn't refresh the cache right now\" for \"the slot survives\" matches the safer outcome for a flaky lock.\n\nNon-Z-Wave exceptions (programming bugs) still propagate -- the softening only covers \\`BaseZwaveJSServerError\\`.\n\n## Type of change\n\n- [ ] Dependency upgrade\n- [x] Bugfix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)\n- [ ] New feature (which adds functionality)\n- [ ] Breaking change (fix/feature causing existing functionality to break)\n- [ ] Code quality improvements to existing code or addition of tests\n\n## Additional information\n\n- This PR fixes or closes issue: fixes #1251\n- This PR is related to issue: follow-up to #1252\n\n## Checklist\n\n- [x] The code change is tested and works locally.\n- [x] There is no commented out code in this PR.\n\nTests:\n- \\`test_uc_v1_set_verify_failure_logs_warning_and_continues\\` -- the new behavior on set\n- \\`test_uc_v1_clear_verify_failure_logs_warning_and_continues\\` -- mirrors the set softening for clear\n- \\`test_uc_v1_verify_non_zwave_error_propagates\\` -- programming bugs still surface\n- (Replaces the prior \\`test_uc_v1_verify_poll_failure_raises_lock_disconnected\\`, which encoded the old contract.)\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Open the PR"
}
```

> AGENT

**PR #1255** opened: https://github.com/raman325/lock_code_manager/pull/1255

## Why one PR, not two

When I tried to cherry-pick `1160f4b0` (the tri-state fix), git reported "nothing to commit" — checking the diff between `facab240` (the 4.0.3 merge commit) and `1160f4b0` showed they're identical. That means **the tri-state fix made it into the squash-merge** and was already shipped in 4.0.3. So only the verify softening needed a new PR.

## What #1255 does

| Layer | Change |
|---|---|
| `_async_uc_verify_write` | `BaseZwaveJSServerError` → log warning + continue (was: raise `LockDisconnected`). Non-Z-Wave exceptions still propagate. |
| Docstring | Spells out the rationale: the SET ack + optimistic push are the safety net; the hourly hard-refresh reconciles drift. |
| Test contract | Old `test_uc_v1_verify_poll_failure_raises_lock_disconnected` deleted; replaced with set-path + clear-path softening tests + non-Z-Wave-propagation test. |

## Verification

- New tests fail pre-fix (2 of 3), pass post-fix.
- Full project suite: 1225 passed.
- pre-commit clean (ruff format pass already applied).

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The verify poll's purpose is *cache hygiene*, not write correctness — the wire-level SET ack is what determines success. By coupling the operation's success to the verify's success, the old code let a transient read failure invalidate a successful write. Decoupling them restores the intuition that "did the SET succeed?" and "did we manage to read it back?" are independent questions.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Ready for review and the next release.

> DEVELOPER

should it be a warning or an info?

> AGENT

Honestly INFO is the better fit. Three reasons:

1. **Frequency.** This path can fire on *every write* to an affected lock. The fallback-activation WARNING at `_uc_fallback_capabilities` fires once per provider lifetime; this would fire dozens of times a day on a lock that rotates PINs. Per-write WARNING noise drowns out signals that need attention.

2. **It's a designed path, not an anomaly.** Once `_uc_fallback` is active for a lock, the verify timing out is the predicted consequence of the same interview pathology that activated the fallback. WARNING reads as "something unexpected happened"; this is expected.

3. **The signal already exists.** The one-shot fallback-activation WARNING ("unified access-control API reports no usable PIN capabilities ... falling back to legacy User Code CC handling") is the maintainer's "this lock is affected by #1251" beacon. The verify failures are downstream of that.

Counter: a user debugging "why is sync flaky?" needs to find this. INFO is gated behind debug logging. But the LCM troubleshooting wiki already directs users to enable debug logging for the integration, so they'd see it.

Let me change it.

> AGENT

The INFO downgrade *did* make it into the merge — both `5e1cbc5` (initial WARNING version) and `32de8e3` (downgrade to INFO) were on the branch when PR #1255 was squashed at 23:24:35 EDT, and the squash includes both. `origin/main` already has `_LOGGER.info` for the verify-failure path.

> DEVELOPER

no I was mistaken. Why didn't you call out this difference when I asked you to do the comparison to LCM 3.x

> AGENT

Honest answer: I scoped the comparison too narrowly. You asked "make sure we have our business logic carried over" and I treated that as "did any 3.x logic go missing?" — which produced a strict 1:1 map. The verify-raises-LockDisconnected behavior **was** preserved 1:1, so it didn't surface as a delta and I marked it ✅.

What I should have done is the second-order question: "given the symptom the user is hitting, is each piece of preserved logic still the right behavior?" The verify-raises code was identical to 3.3.0 — but that's exactly the point. If a user on 3.2.1 was happy and 3.3.0 had the same verify-raises code, then either (a) the verify path didn't fire on their lock back then, or (b) it did and nobody noticed, or (c) some other change between 3.2.1 and 4.0.3 made it newly load-bearing. I should have flagged the verify path as a candidate even though it was preserved, precisely because it's the kind of code that turns flaky-lock transients into hard failures.

The whole comparison would have been more useful framed as a regression hunt rather than a preservation audit. Two different jobs.

Let me save this as feedback so I default to the broader framing next time.

> TOOL

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/raman/.claude/projects/-Users-raman-projects-lock-code-manager/memory/feedback_compare_versions_for_regressions.md",
  "content": "---\nname: feedback-compare-versions-for-regressions\ndescription: When comparing an old release to current code in the context of a user-reported regression, audit preserved logic for symptom-fit too — not just \"is it carried over\"\nmetadata:\n  type: feedback\n---\n\nWhen comparing a prior release (e.g. last 3.x) to current code while a user-reported regression is open, treat the comparison as a **regression hunt**, not just a **preservation audit**.\n\nA preservation audit asks: \"did any old logic go missing?\" — produces a 1:1 map, marks unchanged items ✅, surfaces only deltas.\n\nA regression hunt asks: \"given the symptom, could any preserved-but-unchanged behavior be the culprit?\" — flags identical-to-old code paths as candidates when they're the kind of code that turns a flaky external into a hard failure (verify steps, post-write polls, retry-on-Z-Wave-error wrappers, anything that raises in response to transients).\n\n**Why:** During PR #1252 follow-up I compared v3.3.0 to current `_zwave_js_uc.py` and marked `_async_uc_verify_write` ✅ because it was literally identical to 3.3.0's `_async_verify_write`. Hours later I realized the verify raising `LockDisconnected` on a flaky-interview Schlage was the most likely cause of TheMegamind's \"code slots won't stay enabled\" symptom. Preserved correctly, broken behavior.\n\n**How to apply:** When the user asks for an old-version comparison AND there's an open regression report, after the 1:1 map, add a second pass: for each preserved path, ask \"if this fires on the user's reported lock/device/path, what's the user-visible outcome?\" Flag any path where the answer is \"operation fails / slot suspended / sync loop\" even if it's unchanged. The comparison is incomplete without that pass.\n"
}
```

Memory saved. Short version of the lesson: a comparison-against-an-old-release done while a regression is open needs two passes, not one — first "did anything go missing?" I did the first, skipped the second, and the verify-raises path was exactly the kind of preserved-but-suspect logic that should have surfaced in pass two.

```
1	"""
2	Temporary User Code CC fallback support for the Z-Wave JS provider.
3	
4	Works around issue #1251: on some locks, node-zwave-js's unified
5	access-control API computes degenerate credential capabilities (PIN
6	missing or zero slots) from its cached interview data, and both Home
7	Assistant's ``lock_helpers`` and the driver itself validate every
8	credential write against that data -- bricking PIN management even
9	though the lock works fine through the legacy User Code CC. When the
10	node advertises User Code CC, this layer routes all PIN operations
11	through the legacy User Code CC value paths instead, restoring the
12	pre-4.0 behavior for exactly the population that needs it.
13	
14	The upstream fix is zwave-js/zwave-js#8873. Once the minimum supported
15	driver includes it, the unified API reports usable capabilities for
16	these locks, the detection in ``_uc_fallback_capabilities`` stops
17	triggering, and everything here goes dormant. To remove the fallback
18	entirely at that point:
19	
20	1. Delete this module.
21	2. Make ``ZWaveJSLock`` extend ``BaseLock`` directly again.
22	3. Delete the fallback branch points in ``zwave_js.py`` (grep for
23	   ``_uc_`` and ``_set_in_progress_code_slot``).
24	4. Delete ``tests/providers/zwave_js/test_uc_fallback.py`` and the
25	   ``uc_only_caps_response`` / ``uc_slot_walk`` / ``mock_uc_utils`` /
26	   ``uc_fallback_lock`` fixtures in that package's ``conftest.py``.
27	"""
28	
29	from __future__ import annotations
30	
31	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
32	import functools
33	import logging
34	from typing import Any, Literal
35	
36	from zwave_js_server.const import CommandClass, SetValueStatus
37	from zwave_js_server.const.command_class.lock import (
38	    ATTR_CODE_SLOT,
39	    ATTR_IN_USE,
40	    ATTR_USERCODE,
41	    LOCK_USERCODE_PROPERTY,
42	    LOCK_USERCODE_STATUS_PROPERTY,
43	    CodeSlotStatus,
44	)
45	from zwave_js_server.const.command_class.notification import (
46	    AccessControlNotificationEvent,
47	)
48	from zwave_js_server.exceptions import BaseZwaveJSServerError, NotFoundError
49	from zwave_js_server.model.node import Node
50	from zwave_js_server.util.lock import (
51	    clear_usercode,
52	    get_usercode,
53	    get_usercode_from_node,
54	    get_usercodes,
55	    set_usercode,
56	)
57	
58	from homeassistant.components.zwave_js.const import ATTR_EVENT
59	from homeassistant.core import Event, callback
60	
61	from ..domain.credentials import (
62	    Credential,
63	    CredentialType,
64	    CredentialTypeCapability,
65	    LockCapabilities,
66	    User,
67	)
68	from ..domain.exceptions import (
69	    CodeRejectedError,
70	    LockDisconnected,
71	    LockOperationFailed,
72	)
73	from ..domain.models import SlotCredential
74	from ._base import BaseLock
75	
76	_LOGGER = logging.getLogger(__name__)
77	
78	# SetValueResult statuses that mean a User Code CC value write was accepted.
79	_UC_SET_VALUE_OK = (
80	    SetValueStatus.SUCCESS,
81	    SetValueStatus.SUCCESS_UNSUPERVISED,
82	    SetValueStatus.WORKING,
83	)
84	
85	
86	@dataclass(repr=False, eq=False)
87	class ZWaveJSUserCodeFallbackSupport(BaseLock):
88	    """
89	    User Code CC fallback layer for the Z-Wave JS provider.
90	
91	    Holds every UC-fallback-specific field, helper, and push handler so
92	    the whole workaround can be deleted as a unit (see the module
93	    docstring for the removal recipe). The concrete provider keeps only
94	    the routing branch points that call into this layer.
95	    """
96	
97	    # Whether the unified access-control API is unusable for PIN management
98	    # on this lock and the legacy User Code CC utilities must be used
99	    # instead. None until ``async_get_capabilities`` runs the detection.
100	    # Deliberately stored (not re-probed per operation) so it shares the
101	    # lifetime of the base's capabilities cache: the seam's slot-only
102	    # routing decision is frozen from the same snapshot, and flipping one
103	    # without the other would route writes incoherently. Both reset
104	    # together when the provider is rebuilt (HA restart, LCM reload, or a
105	    # zwave_js entry reload -- which a driver upgrade always triggers),
106	    # so a lock healed by the upstream fix lands back in unified mode on
107	    # its next reload with no LCM change.
108	    _uc_fallback: bool | None = field(init=False, default=None)
109	    # Slot of a UC-fallback set operation currently in flight. User Code CC
110	    # has no in-band duplicate-rejection result; some firmwares report a
111	    # duplicate via an Access Control notification instead (sometimes with
112	    # userId=0). Tracking the in-flight slot lets the notification handler
113	    # attribute that rejection to the right slot.
114	    _set_in_progress_code_slot: int | None = field(init=False, default=None)
115	
116	    @property
117	    def node(self) -> Node:
118	        """Return the Z-Wave JS node; the concrete provider supplies this."""
119	        raise NotImplementedError
120	
121	    def _node_supports_user_code_cc(self) -> bool:
122	        """Return whether the node's endpoint 0 advertises User Code CC."""
123	        return any(cc.id == CommandClass.USER_CODE for cc in self.node.command_classes)
124	
125	    @functools.cached_property
126	    def _usercode_cc_version(self) -> int:
127	        """Return the User Code CC version supported by this node."""
128	        version = next(
129	            (
130	                cc.version
131	                for cc in self.node.command_classes
132	                if cc.id == CommandClass.USER_CODE
133	            ),
134	            0,
135	        )
136	        if version == 0:
137	            _LOGGER.warning(
138	                "Lock %s: User Code CC not found on node %s. This may "
139	                "indicate an incomplete interview. Defaulting to V1 behavior",
140	                self.lock.entity_id,
141	                self.node.node_id,
142	            )
143	            return 1
144	        return version
145	
146	    async def _async_uc_fallback_active(self) -> bool:
147	        """
148	        Return whether PIN operations must use the User Code CC fallback.
149	
150	        The flag is computed by ``async_get_capabilities``; when a
151	        credential operation arrives before any capability probe (e.g. a
152	        direct service call right after a reload), run the probe first so
153	        routing never guesses.
154	        """
155	        if self._uc_fallback is None:
156	            await self._get_cached_capabilities()
157	        return bool(self._uc_fallback)
158	
159	    def _uc_fallback_capabilities(self) -> LockCapabilities | None:
160	        """
161	        Detect the fallback and build slot-only capabilities for it.
162	
163	        Called by ``async_get_capabilities`` after the unified API
164	        reported no usable PIN support. Only falls back when the node
165	        advertises User Code CC -- without it the legacy utilities
166	        cannot work either, and the lock genuinely has no PIN support
167	        LCM can manage -- and when the User Code CC value DB walk finds
168	        slots. Sets ``_uc_fallback`` accordingly and returns None when
169	        no fallback is possible.
170	
171	        ``get_usercodes`` walks slot 1, 2, 3, ... in the value DB until
172	        ``NotFoundError``, so the returned list length is the lock's
173	        actual UC slot count. The function only raises ``NotFoundError``
174	        internally (caught there) and is otherwise pure value-DB
175	        walking, so we let any unexpected exception surface rather
176	        than silently mis-routing the lock to "no PIN support".
177	        """
178	        uc_slots = (
179	            get_usercodes(self.node) if self._node_supports_user_code_cc() else []
180	        )
181	        if not uc_slots:
182	            self._uc_fallback = False
183	            return None
184	        _LOGGER.warning(
185	            "Lock %s: unified access-control API reports no usable PIN "
186	            "capabilities but the node supports User Code CC with %s slots; "
187	            "falling back to legacy User Code CC handling (see issue #1251)",
188	            self.lock.entity_id,
189	            len(uc_slots),
190	        )
191	        self._uc_fallback = True
192	        return LockCapabilities(
193	            # Force slot-only routing: supports_user_management=False
194	            # gates _supports_user_records() at the seam, so the User
195	            # lifecycle (async_set_user / async_delete_user) is skipped
196	            # and our async_set_credential / async_delete_credential
197	            # become the direct call targets.
198	            supports_user_management=False,
199	            max_users=0,
200	            credential_types={
201	                CredentialType.PIN: CredentialTypeCapability(
202	                    num_slots=len(uc_slots),
203	                    # UC spec allows 4-10 ASCII digits per User Code CC v1+.
204	                    min_length=4,
205	                    max_length=10,
206	                    supports_learn=False,
207	                )
208	            },
209	            max_user_name_length=0,
210	        )
211	
212	    @staticmethod
213	    def _uc_slot_state(in_use: bool | None, usercode: str | None) -> SlotCredential:
214	        """
215	        Project a User Code CC slot to a ``SlotCredential``.
216	
217	        Slots count as empty only when ``in_use`` is explicitly ``False``,
218	        or when ``in_use`` is unknown (``None``) with no cached value --
219	        the latter matches the legacy 3.x reader. Masked codes (all
220	        asterisks) and occupied slots without a cached value count as
221	        unreadable; an unknown ``in_use`` with a present value is treated
222	        as occupied so a partially populated cache cannot erase a live
223	        PIN (mirrors the push-path rule at ``_handle_uc_value_update``).
224	        """
225	        if in_use is False:
226	            return SlotCredential.empty()
227	        if not usercode:
228	            return (
229	                SlotCredential.empty()
230	                if in_use is None
231	                else SlotCredential.unreadable()
232	            )
233	        code = str(usercode)
234	        if code == "*" * len(code):
235	            return SlotCredential.unreadable()
236	        return SlotCredential.known(code)
237	
238	    async def _async_uc_users_from_value_db(self) -> list[User]:
239	        """
240	        Synthesize one implicit user per occupied User Code CC slot.
241	
242	        User Code CC has no user records -- the user IS the credential --
243	        so each occupied slot becomes a user with ``user_id == slot``
244	        carrying its single PIN credential. The seam's slot projection
245	        and owner-resolution lookups (untagged user with ``user_id ==
246	        slot`` owning a PIN at ``credential.slot == slot``) match this
247	        shape via their legacy fallback path.
248	
249	        When any managed slot is missing from the value DB or has an
250	        unknown ``in_use`` state, do one hard refresh before projecting
251	        so a partially populated cache is not misread as empty slots.
252	        """
253	        try:
254	            slots = get_usercodes(self.node)
255	        except BaseZwaveJSServerError as err:
256	            raise LockDisconnected(f"get usercodes failed: {err}") from err
257	        slots_by_num = {int(slot[ATTR_CODE_SLOT]): slot for slot in slots}
258	        if any(
259	            slot_num not in slots_by_num
260	            or slots_by_num[slot_num].get(ATTR_IN_USE) is None
261	            for slot_num in self.managed_slots
262	        ):
263	            _LOGGER.debug(
264	                "Lock %s has missing/unknown slots, performing hard refresh",
265	                self.lock.entity_id,
266	            )
267	            await self._async_refresh_usercode_cache()
268	            try:
269	                slots = get_usercodes(self.node)
270	            except BaseZwaveJSServerError as err:
271	                raise LockDisconnected(f"get usercodes failed: {err}") from err
272	
273	        users: list[User] = []
274	        for slot_info in slots:
275	            slot = int(slot_info[ATTR_CODE_SLOT])
276	            state = self._uc_slot_state(
277	                slot_info.get(ATTR_IN_USE), slot_info.get(ATTR_USERCODE)
278	            )
279	            if state.is_empty:
280	                continue
281	            user = User(user_id=slot, name=None, active=True)
282	            user.credentials.append(
283	                Credential(type=CredentialType.PIN, slot=slot, state=state)
284	            )
285	            users.append(user)
286	        return users
287	
288	    async def _async_refresh_usercode_cache(self) -> None:
289	        """Refresh all User Code CC values from the device."""
290	        try:
291	            await self.node.async_refresh_cc_values(CommandClass.USER_CODE)
292	        except BaseZwaveJSServerError as err:
293	            raise LockDisconnected(f"usercode cache refresh failed: {err}") from err
294	
295	    async def _async_uc_set_usercode(self, code_slot: int, usercode: str) -> bool:
296	        """
297	        Write a usercode through the legacy User Code CC value path.
298	
299	        Returns False without writing when the cached value already
300	        matches (masked codes never match, so they are always
301	        rewritten). After a successful write, V1 locks are polled to
302	        force-update the value DB (they don't reliably report back),
303	        and the new state is pushed optimistically so the next sync
304	        tick doesn't read a stale cache and loop.
305	        """
306	        try:
307	            current = get_usercode(self.node, code_slot)
308	        except NotFoundError:
309	            current = None
310	        if current and current.get(ATTR_IN_USE):
311	            current_code = str(current.get(ATTR_USERCODE) or "")
312	            if current_code != "*" * len(current_code) and usercode == current_code:
313	                _LOGGER.debug(
314	                    "Lock %s slot %s already has this PIN, skipping set",
315	                    self.lock.entity_id,
316	                    code_slot,
317	                )
318	                return False
319	
320	        self._set_in_progress_code_slot = code_slot
321	        try:
322	            result = await set_usercode(self.node, code_slot, usercode)
323	        except NotFoundError as err:
324	            self._set_in_progress_code_slot = None
325	            raise CodeRejectedError(
326	                code_slot=code_slot,
327	                lock_entity_id=self.lock.entity_id,
328	                reason=f"slot not found on lock: {err}",
329	            ) from err
330	        except BaseZwaveJSServerError as err:
331	            self._set_in_progress_code_slot = None
332	            raise LockDisconnected(
333	                f"set usercode slot {code_slot} failed: {err}"
334	            ) from err
335	        if result is not None and result.status not in _UC_SET_VALUE_OK:
336	            self._set_in_progress_code_slot = None
337	            raise CodeRejectedError(
338	                code_slot=code_slot,
339	                lock_entity_id=self.lock.entity_id,
340	                reason=f"set value returned {result.status.name}",
341	            )
342	        await self._async_uc_verify_write(code_slot, "set")
343	        # Optimistic update: the value cache updates asynchronously via push
344	        # notification; push now to prevent sync loops from reading stale cache.
345	        self._push_credential_update(code_slot, SlotCredential.known(usercode))
346	        return True
347	
348	    async def _async_uc_clear_usercode(self, code_slot: int) -> bool:
349	        """
350	        Clear a usercode through the legacy User Code CC value path.
351	
352	        Returns False without writing when the slot is already clear.
353	        Mirrors ``_async_uc_set_usercode`` for verification and the
354	        optimistic push.
355	        """
356	        try:
357	            current = get_usercode(self.node, code_slot)
358	        except NotFoundError:
359	            current = None
360	        # Only an explicit in_use=False short-circuits: a missing/None
361	        # ATTR_IN_USE means the cache is partially populated and may hide
362	        # a live PIN, so the clear has to proceed.
363	        if current is not None and current.get(ATTR_IN_USE) is False:
364	            _LOGGER.debug(
365	                "Lock %s slot %s already cleared, skipping clear",
366	                self.lock.entity_id,
367	                code_slot,
368	            )
369	            return False
370	
371	        try:
372	            result = await clear_usercode(self.node, code_slot)
373	        except NotFoundError as err:
374	            raise LockOperationFailed(
375	                f"clear usercode slot {code_slot} failed: {err}"
376	            ) from err
377	        except BaseZwaveJSServerError as err:
378	            raise LockDisconnected(
379	                f"clear usercode slot {code_slot} failed: {err}"
380	            ) from err
381	        if result is not None and result.status not in _UC_SET_VALUE_OK:
382	            raise LockOperationFailed(
383	                f"clear usercode slot {code_slot} failed: "
384	                f"set value returned {result.status.name}"
385	            )
386	        await self._async_uc_verify_write(code_slot, "clear")
387	        # Optimistic update: see _async_uc_set_usercode for rationale.
388	        self._push_credential_update(code_slot, SlotCredential.empty())
389	        return True
390	
391	    async def _async_uc_verify_write(
392	        self, code_slot: int, operation: Literal["set", "clear"]
393	    ) -> None:
394	        """
395	        Force-update the value cache after a set/clear on a V1 lock.
396	
397	        V1 locks don't reliably update the Z-Wave JS value cache after a
398	        write; this poll fires a CC GET directly at the node to force
399	        the value DB to refresh before the next coordinator read.
400	
401	        When the GET itself fails (the canonical case being a Schlage
402	        UC v1 lock whose interview also times out -- issue #1251), log
403	        and continue: the SET/CLEAR already ack'd at the wire and the
404	        caller immediately emits ``_push_credential_update`` with the
405	        true post-write state, so the coordinator already has the
406	        truth. Failing the whole operation here turns a flaky lock into
407	        a slot-suspension feedback loop -- the exact pathology the
408	        fallback is meant to dodge. The hourly hard-refresh backstop
409	        reconciles any genuine cache drift.
410	
411	        Non-Z-Wave exceptions (programming bugs) still propagate.
412	        """
413	        if self._usercode_cc_version >= 2:
414	            return
415	        try:
416	            await get_usercode_from_node(self.node, code_slot)
417	        except BaseZwaveJSServerError as err:
418	            # INFO, not WARNING: the per-lock fallback activation already
419	            # logs a one-shot WARNING; verify timeouts on a flagged lock
420	            # are the predicted consequence of the same interview
421	            # pathology and would spam at WARNING level on every write.
422	            _LOGGER.info(
423	                "Lock %s slot %s: post-%s verification poll failed (%s); "
424	                "trusting optimistic push and continuing",
425	                self.lock.entity_id,
426	                code_slot,
427	                operation,
428	                err,
429	            )
430	
431	    def _uc_code_slot_in_use(self, code_slot: int) -> bool | None:
432	        """Return whether a User Code CC slot is in use, None when unknown."""
433	        try:
434	            return get_usercode(self.node, code_slot)[ATTR_IN_USE]
435	        except NotFoundError, KeyError:
436	            return None
437	
438	    @callback
439	    def _on_uc_value_updated(self, event: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
440	        """Handle ``value updated`` node events for User Code CC values."""
441	        args: dict[str, Any] = event["args"]
442	        if args.get("commandClass") != CommandClass.USER_CODE:
443	            return
444	
445	        property_name = args.get("property")
446	        if property_name not in (
447	            LOCK_USERCODE_PROPERTY,
448	            LOCK_USERCODE_STATUS_PROPERTY,
449	        ):
450	            return
451	
452	        code_slot = int(args["propertyKey"])
453	        # Slot 0 is not a valid user code slot.
454	        if code_slot == 0:
455	            return
456	
457	        # Clear in-progress tracking only on userCode updates for the slot
458	        # we were setting. userIdStatus updates don't confirm acceptance and
459	        # could race with duplicate-code notifications.
460	        if (
461	            property_name == LOCK_USERCODE_PROPERTY
462	            and code_slot == self._set_in_progress_code_slot
463	        ):
464	            self._set_in_progress_code_slot = None
465	
466	        if property_name == LOCK_USERCODE_STATUS_PROPERTY:
467	            self._handle_uc_status_update(code_slot, args.get("newValue"))
468	        else:
469	            self._handle_uc_value_update(code_slot, args.get("newValue"))
470	
471	    @callback
472	    def _handle_uc_status_update(self, code_slot: int, status: Any) -> None:
473	        """Handle a userIdStatus value update for a code slot."""
474	        if status == CodeSlotStatus.AVAILABLE:
475	            # Ignore AVAILABLE status if Lock Code Manager expects a PIN on this
476	            # slot. Some locks send stale AVAILABLE events after a code was set,
477	            # which would cause infinite sync loops.
478	            if (
479	                self.coordinator
480	                and self.coordinator.desired_credential(code_slot).is_present
481	            ):
482	                _LOGGER.debug(
483	                    "Lock %s: ignoring userIdStatus=AVAILABLE for slot %s "
484	                    "(LCM expects PIN on this slot)",
485	                    self.lock.entity_id,
486	                    code_slot,
487	                )
488	                return
489	
490	            # Slot was cleared - update coordinator if needed
491	            current = self.coordinator.data.get(code_slot) if self.coordinator else None
492	            if self.coordinator and (current is None or not current.is_empty):
493	                _LOGGER.debug(
494	                    "Lock %s: slot %s userIdStatus=AVAILABLE, marking cleared",
495	                    self.lock.entity_id,
496	                    code_slot,
497	                )
498	                self._push_credential_update(code_slot, SlotCredential.empty())
499	
500	    @callback
501	    def _handle_uc_value_update(self, code_slot: int, new_value: Any) -> None:
502	        """Handle a userCode value update for a code slot."""
503	        if not new_value:
504	            resolved = SlotCredential.empty()
505	        else:
506	            value = str(new_value)
507	            slot_in_use = self._uc_code_slot_in_use(code_slot)
508	            # Asymmetric in_use checks: masked codes count as unreadable even
509	            # when in_use is None (some firmwares mask before reporting
510	            # status), but all-zeros only counts as empty when in_use is
511	            # explicitly False (zeros from a partially-loaded cache must
512	            # not be misread as cleared).
513	            if value == "*" * len(value) and slot_in_use is not False:
514	                resolved = SlotCredential.unreadable()
515	            elif value.strip("0") == "" and slot_in_use is False:
516	                resolved = SlotCredential.empty()
517	            else:
518	                resolved = SlotCredential.known(value)
519	
520	        # Z-Wave JS sends duplicate events; skip if the value is unchanged.
521	        if self.coordinator and self.coordinator.data.get(code_slot) == resolved:
522	            return
523	
524	        _LOGGER.debug(
525	            "Lock %s received push update for slot %s: %s",
526	            self.lock.entity_id,
527	            code_slot,
528	            "****" if resolved.is_readable else f"({resolved.as_label()})",
529	        )
530	        self._push_credential_update(code_slot, resolved)
531	
532	    @callback
533	    def _uc_handle_duplicate_notification(self, evt: Event, code_slot: int) -> bool:
534	        """
535	        Attribute a duplicate-code notification to the in-flight set, if any.
536	
537	        Only fires when LCM initiated the set (the in-progress slot is
538	        only tracked by the UC-fallback write path; unified-mode writes
539	        report duplicates in-band). Marks the slot as rejected so the
540	        sync manager raises DuplicateCodeError on the next tick, routing
541	        through the standard CodeRejectedError flow. Some Z-Wave lock
542	        firmwares report this notification with userId=0 instead of the
543	        offending slot; treat 0 as referring to the slot we're currently
544	        setting. Returns True when the notification was consumed.
545	        """
546	        if (
547	            evt.data[ATTR_EVENT]
548	            == AccessControlNotificationEvent.NEW_USER_CODE_NOT_ADDED_DUE_TO_DUPLICATE_CODE
549	            and self._set_in_progress_code_slot is not None
550	            and code_slot in (0, self._set_in_progress_code_slot)
551	        ):
552	            slot = self._set_in_progress_code_slot
553	            self._set_in_progress_code_slot = None
554	            self.mark_code_rejected(slot)
555	            return True
556	        return False
557
```

```
178	    def _get_all_usercodes(self) -> ServiceResponse:
```

```
    async def async_set_value(
        self,
        val: Value | str,
        new_value: Any,
        options: dict | None = None,
        wait_for_result: bool | None = None,
    ) -> SetValueResult | None:
        """Send setValue command to Node for given value (or value_id)."""
        # a value may be specified as value_id or the value itself
        if not isinstance(val, Value):
            if val not in self.values:
                raise NotFoundError(f"Value {val} not found on node {self}")
            val = self.values[val]

        if val.metadata.writeable is False:
            raise UnwriteableValue

        cmd_args = {
            "valueId": _get_value_id_dict_from_value_data(val.data),
            "value": new_value,
        }
        if options:
            option = next(
                (
                    option
                    for option in options
                    if option not in val.metadata.value_change_options
                ),
                None,
            )
            if option is not None:
                raise NotFoundError(
                    f"Option {option} not found on value {val} on node {self}"
                )
            cmd_args["options"] = options

        # the value object needs to be send to the server
        result = await self.async_send_command(
            "set_value", **cmd_args, require_schema=29, wait_for_result=wait_for_result
        )

        if result is None:
            return None

        return SetValueResult(result["result"])
```

> AGENT

I have the data. Here's the actual answer — the comparison done as a regression hunt this time.

## 4.0.4 UC fallback path vs. 3.3.0 implementation

The fallback in 4.0.4 (`_zwave_js_uc.py`) is the moral equivalent of 3.3.0's `zwave_js.py`. Most logic is preserved; here's every place the **observable behavior** diverges, in priority order:

### 🔴 Differs in a way that can cause "slots won't stay enabled"

**`_async_uc_set_usercode` / `_async_uc_clear_usercode`: check `SetValueResult.status` and raise on non-OK**

```python
# 4.0.4 — _zwave_js_uc.py:335 (set) and 381 (clear)
if result is not None and result.status not in _UC_SET_VALUE_OK:
    raise CodeRejectedError(...)      # set path
    raise LockOperationFailed(...)    # clear path
```

`_UC_SET_VALUE_OK = (SUCCESS, SUCCESS_UNSUPERVISED, WORKING)`. Anything else (`FAIL=2`, `NO_DEVICE_SUPPORT=0`, `ENDPOINT_NOT_FOUND=3`, `NOT_IMPLEMENTED=4`, `INVALID_VALUE=5`) escalates to a rejection. **The sync layer treats `CodeRejectedError` as a slot-level rejection and disables the slot.**

3.3.0 routed through the HA service `zwave_js.set_lock_usercode`. I just read the current implementation of that service — `homeassistant/components/zwave_js/lock.py:132`:

```python
async def async_set_lock_usercode(self, code_slot: int, usercode: str) -> None:
    try:
        await set_usercode(self.info.node, code_slot, usercode)
    except BaseZwaveJSServerError as err:
        raise HomeAssistantError(...) from err
```

That service **does not inspect `SetValueResult.status` at all.** It silently treats `FAIL` / `NO_DEVICE_SUPPORT` / etc. the same as `SUCCESS`. The result is discarded. So 3.3.0's behavior on a lock that returns `FAIL` from a SET: HA service returns silently → 3.3.0 runs verify → optimistic-pushes `known(usercode)` → returns True → slot stays enabled in LCM's view.

4.0.4 on the same wire response: `CodeRejectedError` → slot disabled.

This is the regression. **A flaky lock that occasionally returns a non-OK status will be silently tolerated by 3.3.0 and aggressively disabled by 4.0.4.** Schlage UC v1 locks with the interview-timeout pathology that AlCalzone diagnosed for TheMegamind are exactly the population that would emit transient non-OK statuses.

### 🟡 Other differences — unlikely root causes, worth noting

- **Direct `set_usercode` / `clear_usercode` instead of HA service.** Intentional — bypasses `lock_helpers` validation that caused #1251. **This is the fix, not a regression.**
- **Verify catches broader exception class** (`BaseZwaveJSServerError` vs `FailedZWaveCommand`). 4.0.4 is *more* tolerant. Not a regression.
- **Verify is non-fatal (post-PR #1255).** 4.0.4 logs INFO and continues; 3.3.0 raised `LockDisconnected`. 4.0.4 is *more* tolerant. Not a regression.
- **Cache short-circuit catches `NotFoundError` only** (was bare `except Exception`). I verified `get_usercode` only raises `NotFoundError`, so equivalent in practice.
- **Tri-state `_uc_slot_state`.** 4.0.4 distinguishes `in_use=None` from `in_use=False`. For `(None, "****")`, 4.0.4 returns `unreadable` vs. 3.3.0's `empty`. Could change sync decisions on partial caches, but not the direct culprit — `unreadable` is treated as "in sync" by the seam, so this would *prevent* loops, not cause them.
- **Clear short-circuit narrower** (`is False` only). Causes 4.0.4 to issue more clears against ambiguous cache state. Clears are idempotent on the lock; harmless.

### 🟢 Confirmed identical

- `_usercode_cc_version` detection + V1 warning
- Cache short-circuit on identical PIN (skip if `in_use=True` and code matches, masked never matches)
- Optimistic push after verify (`known(usercode)` for set, `empty()` for clear)
- Push subscription to `value updated` events
- `_handle_uc_value_update` / `_handle_uc_status_update` (asymmetric in_use rule, stale AVAILABLE ignore)
- Duplicate-code notification attribution via `_set_in_progress_code_slot`

## What to do

The fix matching 3.3.0 semantics is to **stop raising on non-OK `SetValueResult.status`**. Options:

1. **Drop the check entirely** — match 3.3.0 exactly. Optimistic push + sync comparison is the safety net for divergence.
2. **Demote to a log + continue** — same shape as the verify softening we just did. Loud signal in logs, but the operation isn't failed.
3. **Narrow `_UC_SET_VALUE_OK`'s complement to truly fatal statuses** — only raise on `NO_DEVICE_SUPPORT` / `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` / `ENDPOINT_NOT_FOUND` (structural impossibilities), tolerate `FAIL` / `INVALID_VALUE` (transient).

I'd lean **option 2** — same pattern as the verify softening, keeps `CodeRejectedError` available for the genuinely unrecoverable cases via option 3's filter. Concretely:

```python
if result is not None and result.status in _UC_SET_VALUE_FATAL:
    raise CodeRejectedError(...)   # structural: NO_DEVICE_SUPPORT, NOT_IMPLEMENTED, ENDPOINT_NOT_FOUND
elif result is not None and result.status not in _UC_SET_VALUE_OK:
    _LOGGER.info(
        "Lock %s slot %s: set returned %s; trusting optimistic push",
        self.lock.entity_id, code_slot, result.status.name,
    )
```

That way a `FAIL` is logged but the slot survives, while a truly impossible operation still fails loudly.

Want me to draft that as a PR now, or wait for TheMegamind's logs to confirm a non-OK status is what's actually firing?
